# Continuity

## Now

- 2026-07-13 longform event vertical slice is implemented. The canonical
  `npm run full-pipeline` / `scripts/editorial-pipeline.ts` route and the legacy
  `/triage` + `/blueprint` route recognize `profile_hint: longform-event`, build
  transcript-bounded chronological chapters, persist cut exclusions and coverage
  in canonical artifacts, fail closed outside the 85–115% duration gate, and
  rejoin the existing compiler. A read-only Lively ALT smoke retained all nine
  non-overlapping source files and reduced 168.2 minutes to 62.7 minutes /
  136 clips / 29 chapters, compiling the timeline in 147 ms. A real one-hour render and chapter-sampled
  visual QA are still outstanding, so the broader full-auto goal remains open.
- 2026-07-10 productization audit: `Dev` is at `8dd88144`. GitHub Actions run
  `29063263116` passes repo-hygiene, agent-definitions, editor-server, and
  schema-contract, but fails macos-studio on Swift 5.10 source compatibility and
  node-runtime on a clean-install dependency boundary. Local Swift 6.2.4 tests pass
  516/516 and local `npm run build` passes, confirming environment/boundary drift
  rather than a generally broken local checkout.
- 2026-07-10 CI Gate 0 is complete on `Dev` at `08a39f53`. GitHub Actions run
  `29073236878` passes all six jobs: repo-hygiene, agent-definitions,
  editor-server, schema-contract, macos-studio, and node-runtime. The repair covers
  Swift 5.10 syntax/concurrency compatibility, deterministic Japanese collection
  ordering, root/editor dependency isolation, self-contained timeline fixtures,
  and explicit FFmpeg installation for hosted media integration tests. Evidence is
  in `reports/ci-baseline-repair-20260710.md`.
- 2026-07-10 product Gate 1 is complete. The normative contract at
  `docs/speech-led-highlight-product-contract.md` fixes the first-run route to
  `interview-highlight` / `interview`, requires an actual 60–180 second rough cut,
  reuses the existing project state and canonical artifacts, and defines degraded
  behavior, exclusions, and editor-effort / NLE-revision metrics. The next P0 is one
  rights-cleared, operator-approved speech-led golden.
- 2026-07-10 product Gate 2 is complete. `lively-alt-vol5` timeline v2 is
  rights-confirmed, operator-approved, and discovered as `tier=human`. The local
  private golden is 91.208 seconds with Japanese captions, -15.8 LUFS mastering,
  and a source-audio-synchronized alternate closing angle; source footage and
  renders remain untracked. Marlin real-model QA timed out and is not counted as a
  passing regression. Evidence is in
  `reports/project-control-loop/2026-07-10-lively-alt-vol5-human-golden.md`.
- 2026-07-10 product Gate 3 is complete. The operator-approved v3 uses 60 px
  Japanese captions and finishes at stable `packaged` state with zero stale
  artifacts. Final MP4 is 91.333333 seconds at 24 fps / 48 kHz, all 9 package QA
  checks pass, Studio exact preview is `ready` with 18 captions and warning 0, and
  Premiere XML dry-run import maps 12/12 clips with zero diff. The PC restart
  resumed at Gate 9 without re-running analysis. Marlin remains fail-closed for
  T-0016. Evidence is in
  `reports/project-control-loop/2026-07-10-speech-led-resumable-e2e.md`.
- The 2026-06-05 “advanced prototype, not one product” headline still holds, but
  state machine, runtime pipeline ownership, deterministic patching, direct Studio
  editing, golden evaluation, visual QA, and NLE human revision diffs are already

  human-approved E2E golden proof.
- Canonical plan: `docs/video-os-v2-productization-audit-20260710.md`. Preserve

The current M6.5 implementation also includes timeline-scoped AI consultation prompt handoff, active named Source Bin collections, transition preset label consistency, Source Bin hover skim, transition edit-point hover preview/click wells, manual edit save-state copy, explicit single-clip occupied target lane lift, timeline keyboard clip selection, timeline edit-point keyboard navigation, manual timeline navigation reveal, a timeline overview visible viewport window, timeline overview scrub snap cues, a timeline ruler playhead HUD, timeline track/marker header semantics, timeline role color semantics, grouped icon-first timeline edit toolbar density, active transition preset drag status, a compact source-bin filter menu, source-bin usage grouping, and Source Bin thumbnail browsing, so the top-level editing-feel scope now covers repeated manual editing, transition placement, material curation, timeline orientation, magnetic whole-timeline navigation, playhead position reading, and AI-assisted edit discussion without crowding the editor chrome.

The latest M6.5 Clip Inspector annotation draft state slice makes the editor-note handoff loop explicit after a read-only Agent consultation is pinned. `ProjectEditorAnnotationDraftState` compares note/handoff drafts with the saved annotation, calls out read-only Agent PREVIEW notes, disables saving unchanged drafts, and keeps Clip Inspector copy clear that `editor_annotations.json` is written only when the editor saves while `timeline.json` stays untouched.

The previous M6.5 Agent result handoff slice adds a manual bridge from read-only AI consultation output back into the editor's selected-clip note drafts. `TimelineAgentResultHandoffDraft` marks the assistant output as `読み取り専用/PREVIEW`, truncates long responses, appends to existing note/handoff drafts instead of overwriting them, and `StudioViewModel.pinSelectedAgentTurnToClipNoteDraft()` updates only draft strings without saving `editor_annotations.json` or mutating `timeline.json`.

The previous M6.5 timeline-scoped AI consultation slice adds an Agent-panel bridge from selected timeline clips/transitions to a read-only prompt. `TimelineAgentConsultationPrompt` captures selected clip IDs, timecodes, source ranges, roles, motivations, existing notes, transition type/duration/handles, and available evidence, while `StudioViewModel.prepareTimelineSelectionAgentPrompt()` fills the Agent prompt without starting a write job or mutating `timeline.json`.

The previous M6.5 named Source Bin collection slice adds a lightweight project-scoped selection bin on top of the existing `作業bin`, favorite, used/unused, thumbnail, hover skim, quick insert, and drag-to-timeline paths. `ProjectMediaSourceBinFilter.collection` filters through the same Source Bin summary/search/sort/grouping contract, while `StudioViewModel.mediaSourceBinActiveCollectionName` and `mediaSourceBinCollectionAssetIDsByProject` persist the active collection name and its asset IDs in UserDefaults without changing `timeline.json`, review patch schema, compiler schema, source insert/drop operations, or project artifacts.

The latest M6.5 transition preset label consistency slice removes the remaining English shorthand from transition preset controls. `TimelineTransitionPreset.localizedLabel` now returns `クロスフェード`, `黒へディップ`, and `ソフトカット`, so the Timeline palette chip, active drag pill, recommended/nearby/drop cue labels, hover/drop preview labels, help text, and accessibility labels all read from the same Japanese names.

This is a UI label-only change. `transition_type`, `applied_skill_id`, `set_transition`, timeline.json, review patch schema, compiler schema, transition planning, duration dragging, relocation, click-to-apply, and drag/drop commit paths are unchanged. `TimelineTransitionDropPlanTests` now fixes the Japanese labels alongside the existing planner tests.

The previous M6.5 Source Bin hover skim slice makes source-bin thumbnails react before click, insert, or drag. `MediaSourceBinRow` and `MediaSourceBinTile` use `onContinuousHover` on the thumbnail area to call `StudioViewModel.previewSourceBinSkim(assetID:fraction:)`, which maps the pointer x-position through `ProjectMediaSkimPreviewTime` into the ready asset or best-candidate source range and sends that frame to the Viewer with a `SOURCE SKIM` time label.

The hover skim path is view-only. It does not set `sourceMonitorAssetID`, does not change insert candidate selection, and clears when hover exits, playback starts, timeline skim begins, source monitor selection is confirmed, clip/transition move previews start, or the timeline/playhead state changes. Source insert, quick drag, `timeline.json`, review patch schema, and compiler schema are unchanged.

The previous M6.5 transition edit-point hover preview slice makes an empty video/overlay seam react before click or drop. `TimelineTransitionDropTarget` now starts a default crossfade preview when the pointer enters the visible central well, reusing `StudioViewModel.previewTransitionPreset(...)` and `TimelineTransitionDropPlan` so the Viewer, seam label, magnet cue, playhead boundary seek, and status copy match the existing drag-hover preview path.

The hover path is owned separately from drag/drop preview state. It uses the default crossfade preset, says `クリックすると適用します。` instead of `離すと適用します。`, clears on hover exit/disappear or after the seam becomes an actual transition, and only starts when no preset drag, transition move drag, or duration drag is active. This does not change `set_transition`, `timeline.json`, review patch schema, compiler schema, or commit contracts.

The previous M6.5 transition edit-point click well slice makes an empty video/overlay seam actionable before the editor starts a drag. `TimelineTransitionPreset.defaultPreset` now names crossfade as the default transition, and `TimelineTransitionDropTarget.editPointClickGesture` applies that default through the existing seam-specific `onApplyTransitionPreset` path when the short click lands inside the visible central well. Existing transition tap selection, center-handle relocation, duration dragging, preset drag/drop, lane-level magnetic drop, `Command-T`, `timeline.json`, review patch schema, compiler schema, and `set_transition` operation contracts are unchanged.

The previous M6.5 manual edit save-state copy slice removes the remaining "apply later" feeling from the hand-edit persistence controls. `FeedbackStatusBar.ApplyAndPreviewButton`, `Timeline.EditToolbar.ApplyPatch`, the timeline command menu, and `StudioViewModel.applyStudioPatch` status copy now describe the action as saving already-visible Studio edits to `timeline.json` and updating preview, rather than applying edits to the timeline. This is UI copy only and does not change immediate Timeline/Viewer updates, patch serialization, `review_patch.json` handling, compiler schema, or timeline operation contracts.

The previous M6.5 explicit clip vertical drag lane-lift slice makes single-clip vertical moves match the already-implemented group lane-lift behavior. `TimelineClipMovePlan.make(...)` now resolves an explicitly requested occupied compatible target track to an open same-kind track, or to a newly created next track ID when no open lane exists, instead of returning `nil` and showing a blocked state. This keeps incompatible-track rejection, caption rejection, same-track magnetic displacement, group move behavior, `move_segment.target_track_id`, `timeline.json`, review patch schema, and compiler schema unchanged.

The previous M6.5 timeline skim preview slice adds FCPX-like passive timeline inspection. `TimelineTrackRow` uses `onContinuousHover` on clip bodies and empty lanes to call `StudioViewModel.previewTimelineSkim(...)`, `activeViewerFrame` prefers the transient skim frame after clip-move preview and before the real playhead, and `Timeline.SkimPreview.*` / `Timeline.ClipSkimCue.*` show a cyan skimmer line, `SKIM` badge, and clip cue while Viewer media/timecode follows the hover frame. Skimming is view-only and clears for source monitor, playback, playhead scrub, clip selection, transition selection, drag move, trim, blade, source drop, transition drag, and timeline reload; it does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch schema, compiler schema, Core planner, or edit operation paths.

The previous M6.5 timeline overview visible viewport window slice makes zoomed detail editing keep a visible whole-sequence context. `TimelinePanel` reads the detail `ScrollView` content frame via `TimelineScrollContentFramePreferenceKey`, `TimelineViewportScale.visibleFrameRange(...)` maps the current horizontal lane offset to timeline frames, and `TimelineOverviewCanvas` draws `Timeline.Overview.ViewportWindow` over the overview while the overview accessibility value includes the visible timecode range. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, scrub resolution, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 timeline overview scrub snap cue slice makes whole-timeline navigation show the same magnetic feedback as ruler and empty-lane scrub. `TimelineOverviewStrip` keeps `activeScrubSnap`, `TimelineOverviewCanvas` draws the shared snap indicator on the overview strip, and `Timeline.Overview.ScrubPreview` adds a compact `SNAP` badge plus snap label help/accessibility during active overview scrub. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, scrub resolution, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 timeline ruler playhead HUD slice keeps the current timecode readable directly on the ruler after scrub gestures end. `TimelineRulerPlayheadBadge` shows the idle playhead timecode at `Timeline.Ruler.PlayheadTimecode`, while the existing `Timeline.RulerScrubPreview` and magnetic snap indicator stay in front during an active ruler scrub. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, scrub resolution, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 timeline track/marker header semantics slice makes the left timeline gutter explain lane kind without requiring the editor to infer it from `V1` / `A1` / `O1` / `C1` / `M`. `TimelineTrackHeader` shows a kind icon, track ID, and short Japanese kind label, `TimelineMarkerLaneHeader` labels the marker lane, and `timelineTrackHeaderWidth` keeps playhead anchors, marker lane, and track rows aligned. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 timeline role color semantics slice makes the overview strip, clip fill, and clip role badge share one role color mapping. `TimelineOverviewRoleLegend` shows only the roles currently used in the loaded timeline, and `TimelineClipBlock` now shows a compact color-dot role badge, using an abbreviation for very narrow clips. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 timeline edit toolbar grouped clusters slice keeps the icon-first density but adds visual command regions. `TimelineEditToolbar.toolbarSection` groups existing controls into mode, transition, selection edit, trim, edit-point/source-range, source, and session clusters with subtle background/stroke separation plus section accessibility identifiers. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 timeline edit toolbar icon-first density slice makes repeated edit commands read more like a pro tool strip than a long Japanese text-button row. `TimelineEditToolbar.toolbarButton` now renders command buttons as fixed 24x22pt icon-only controls, while keeping the original title as `accessibilityLabel` and preserving caller-provided help, disabled states, actions, and accessibility identifiers. This is UI-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, compiler contracts, or any edit operation path.

The previous M6.5 source-bin compact filter menu slice replaces the crowded eight-option segmented filter with a menu picker plus selected-condition summary. `MediaPanel.SourceBinFilter` keeps the existing accessibility identifier while using a `.menu` picker, and `MediaPanel.SourceBinFilterSummary` shows the active filter and count. This is UI-only and does not touch `ProjectMediaSourceBinFilter`, `StudioViewModel` persistence, `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 source-bin usage grouping slice lets editors keep the material shelf visible while separating unused media from media already in the cut. `ProjectMediaSourceBinGroupMode.usage` and `ProjectMediaPreviewSummary.groupedItems(... usedAssetIDs:)` split the current filtered/searched/sorted source rows into `unused` and `used` groups, with unused rows first for continuing an AI rough assembly. This stays view-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The earlier M6.5 source-bin used/unused filter slice helps editors continue from an AI rough assembly without manually remembering which media is already on the timeline. `ProjectMediaSourceBinFilter.used` / `.unused` use `StudioViewModel.mediaSourceBinUsedAssetIDs`, derived from the current in-memory timeline, and `MediaPanel.SourceBinUsedBadge.*` marks rows already in the cut. This stays view-only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 aligned cross-track magnetic ripple delete slice lets editors delete a shared selected range across V/A/O tracks and close that range on each affected track. `TimelineRippleDeleteGroupPlan` now records `trackIDs`, `rangeInFrame`, `rangeOutFrame`, and `isCrossTrackRipple`; when every selected track covers the same continuous range and no unselected clip overlaps it, the plan emits existing `remove_segment` plus downstream `move_segment` operations per affected track. Misaligned cross-track selections still fall back to lift delete through the normal delete path, and no timeline/review/compiler schema changes were needed.

The earlier M6.5 source-bin favorites filter slice makes the Media-panel material shelf more useful after AI selection. `ProjectMediaSourceBinFilter.favorites` and `ProjectMediaPreviewSummary.items/count/groupedItems(... favoriteAssetIDs:)` let the existing filter/search/sort/group pipeline narrow to starred assets, while `StudioViewModel.mediaSourceBinFavoriteAssetIDsByProject` stores project-scoped favorites in `UserDefaults`. `MediaPanel.SourceBinFavoriteButton.*` adds a star to each source row and `★` appears in the source-bin filter without touching `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 Source Monitor append-to-end shortcut slice adds the FCPX-like `E` append path. `StudioViewModel.appendSourceMonitorToTimelineEnd()` reuses `TimelineSourceInsertPlan.make(...)` with `playheadFrame: timeline.totalFrames`, so the same `insert_segment`, immediate Timeline/Viewer update, selected inserted-clip state, and changed-clip highlight path applies without moving the playhead first. `StudioCommandPaletteCommand`, `ContentView.commandItems`, and `SourceMonitorShortcutButtons` expose the action through Command Palette and Source Monitor focus `E` without touching `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 Source Monitor edit action shortcuts slice makes source-to-timeline editing less mouse-heavy. `StudioCommandPaletteCommand` now exposes source insert, overwrite, and selected-clip replace commands, `ContentView.commandItems` routes them to the existing immediate `StudioViewModel` paths, and `SourceMonitorShortcutButtons` handles Source Monitor focus `W` / `D` / `R` for insert/overwrite/replace without colliding with timeline-focus trim/loop shortcuts or text input. This adds action routing only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 source-bin smart bin grouping slice makes the Media-panel source bin less like one flat list and closer to an editorial material browser. `ProjectMediaPreviewSummary.groupedItems` now groups filtered/searched/sorted source rows by flat list, folder, playback status, or media kind; `StudioViewModel.mediaSourceBinGroupMode` persists the chosen grouping via `UserDefaults`; and `MediaPanel.SourceBinGroupModePicker` renders group headers while reusing the same row component for source monitor preview, quick insert, and source-row drag. This adds view grouping only and does not touch `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 persistent source-bin search and source monitor context slice keeps the editor's source selection context across launches. `StudioViewModel` now loads/saves `mediaSourceBinQuery`, `sourceMonitorAssetID`, and `sourceMonitorCandidateID` via `UserDefaults`, restores the context on initial default project selection, and clears it only when the persisted asset is not ready in the selected project, the editor returns to Program preview, or an actual project switch happens. This preserves source-bin search and source monitor candidate context without touching `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 persistent timeline viewport preferences slice keeps the editor's timeline working view across launches. `StudioViewModel` now loads/saves `timelinePixelsPerFrame`, `isTimelineFitToWindowEnabled`, and `timelineTrackDensity` via `UserDefaults`, matching existing snapping and source-bin filter/sort persistence. This preserves zoom/detail density without touching `timeline.json`, review patch, or compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 visible delete versus ripple delete toolbar slice exposes the already-implemented safe delete path directly in `TimelineEditToolbar`. The new `削除` button calls `StudioViewModel.deleteTimelineSelection()`, so same-track clip selections ripple-delete, cross-track clip selections lift-delete while preserving gaps, and selected transitions return to cut from the same visible toolbar action. The specialized `リップル削除` button remains same-track gap-close only and now uses a gap-closing arrow icon so it is visually distinct from normal delete.

The previous M6.5 same-track multi-selection ripple toolbar slice aligns the visible toolbar entry with the already-implemented group ripple delete path. `TimelineEditToolbar` now enables `リップル削除` whenever clips are selected and `canRippleDeleteSelectedClip` is true, so same-track multi-selections can use the toolbar while cross-track selections remain disabled for toolbar ripple. The help copy now explains cumulative downstream movement for multi-clip ripple deletes.

The previous M6.5 multi-selection range summary slice makes group selection scope visible before destructive or range-based group actions. `TimelinePanel.selectedClipSelections(in:)` now rebuilds the selected `TimelineClipSelection` array from the current selection IDs, and `TimelineEditToolbar` uses it to show clip count, target tracks, selected range, duration, and the primary clip in summary/help copy. Multi-selection summary width expands only in the multi-select state, preserving the denser single-clip and transition toolbar layout.

The previous M6.5 default transition palette affordance slice makes the crossfade preset visibly and accessibly read as the default transition. `TimelineTransitionPresetChip` now adds a compact command-key icon only for `.crossfade`, extends the tooltip with the `Command-T` default apply path, and exposes a default-transition accessibility label/hint while leaving click, drag/drop, Command Palette, and shortcut application on the existing quick-apply/drop path.

The previous M6.5 default crossfade shortcut slice makes the FCPX-like default transition path available from Command Palette and timeline-focus `Command-T`. `StudioCommandPaletteCommand` now exposes `apply-default-crossfade-transition` with crossfade/default/Command-T/FCP/edit-point search terms, `ContentView.commandItems` routes it through `applyTransitionPresetNearContext(TimelineTransitionPreset.crossfade.id)`, and `TimelineShortcutButtons` keeps `Command-T` focus-gated so it does not collide with text entry or source monitor focus.

The previous M6.5 selected-transition duration shortcut slice makes the 0.5-second transition shorten/lengthen action available from Command Palette and timeline-focus `Shift-[` / `Shift-]`, not only the toolbar. `StudioCommandPaletteCommand` now exposes `shorten-selected-transition` / `lengthen-selected-transition` with searchable transition/duration Japanese and English keywords, `ContentView.commandItems` routes them through the existing selected-transition duration nudge path with disabled reasons, and `TimelineShortcutButtons` keeps the shortcut focus-gated so it does not collide with text entry or source monitor focus.

The previous M6.5 selected-transition duration nudge slice lets editors shorten or lengthen the currently selected applied transition by 0.5 seconds from the timeline edit toolbar. `StudioViewModel.shortenSelectedTimelineTransitionDuration` / `lengthenSelectedTimelineTransitionDuration` compute the sequence-based frame step, guard against handle min/max limits, and reuse `adjustTimelineTransitionDuration` so the same `set_transition` patch, in-memory timeline update, selected-transition state, playhead seek, Viewer fade preview, and status path apply before save/update. `TimelineEditToolbar` now shows `長さ−` / `長さ＋` next to the transition preset palette and disables each button at the relevant limit.

The previous M6.5 source bin quick-insert slice lets editors add a ready Media-panel row's visible best candidate to the playhead without opening the source monitor or dragging. `MediaSourceBinRow` now exposes an icon-only quick insert button beside source preview, and `StudioViewModel.insertSourceBinQuickCandidateAtPlayhead` reuses `sourceBinQuickDragCandidate` so the button, row cue, and drag payload resolve to the same candidate. The commit path queues the same `insert_segment` review operation, immediately updates the in-memory timeline, selects/highlights the inserted clip, seeks the playhead to the insert point, and bumps media/audio sync generation so Viewer follows the result before save/update.

The previous M6.5 source bin quick-drag candidate cue slice makes ready Media-panel rows show what will be dragged before the editor starts the drag. `StudioViewModel.sourceBinQuickDragSummary` now shares the same `sourceBinQuickDragCandidate` resolver as `sourceBinQuickDragPayload`, so the visible segment, role, default target track, duration, and confidence match the actual source candidate payload. `MediaSourceBinRow` shows that metadata only when the row is ready and has a valid candidate, avoiding misleading drag affordances for missing or candidate-less media.

The earlier M6.5 source bin quick-drag slice lets editors drag a ready Media-panel source row directly onto the timeline without first opening the source monitor candidate card. `StudioViewModel.sourceBinQuickDragPayload` resolves the row to the best unused `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` candidate and emits the existing `StudioDragPayload.sourceCandidate` payload. `MediaSourceBinRow` only exposes the drag provider and hand cue when the asset is ready and has a valid candidate, so the existing source-drop ghost, snap, occupied-lane lift, immediate Timeline update, and Viewer sync path stay shared with source candidate card drag.

The previous M6.5 immediate toolbar trim suite makes the non-drag trim controls behave like direct manipulation instead of delayed patch notes. `StudioViewModel.queueAndApplyTimelineTrimOperations` now queues existing `trim_segment` / `move_segment` operations for save/update and applies the same operations to the in-memory timeline immediately, clearing transient previews and syncing Program Viewer media/audio. The fixed 0.5s start/end trim buttons now reuse `TimelineDragTrimPlan`, and roll, extend, and slip toolbar actions use the same immediate Timeline/Viewer update path.

The previous M6.5 trim-to-playhead shortcut slice lets editors set the playhead inside a selected clip and press `Q` / `W`, use Command Palette, or use the existing toolbar edge actions to trim the selected clip's start/end to that playhead. `StudioViewModel.trimSelectedTimelineClipToPlayhead` now applies `TimelinePlayheadTrimPlan` operations to the in-memory timeline immediately, clears transient source/transition/drag previews, preserves the patch queue for save/update, and syncs Program Viewer media/audio before save. `ContentView` exposes focus-gated `Q` / `W` shortcuts plus Command Palette entries with explicit immediate Timeline/Viewer copy.

The previous M6.5 selected group occupied-lane lift slice lets a multi-selected group from one source track move up or down into an occupied compatible target and then lift together to an open or newly-created compatible track. `TimelineClipGroupMovePlan` now carries `laneLift`, reuses the existing `move_segment.target_track_id` contract for every selected clip, records overlapped clip IDs, and still rejects incompatible or cross-track group targets. `TimelineTrackRow` shows the open/new target cue, source-row ghost, landing cue, and target label before mouse-up, while `StudioViewModel` reports whether the group was lifted to an existing track or a newly-created one.

The previous M6.5 same-track selected group vertical-move slice lets a multi-selected group from one source track move up or down to an open compatible target track while preserving timing. `TimelineClipGroupMovePlan` accepts `preferredTargetTrackID`, limits target-track group moves to selections that all originate on the same source track, emits existing `move_segment.target_track_id` operations, and leaves occupied compatible targets to the newer lane-lift path while keeping incompatible or cross-track group targets blocked. `StudioViewModel` passes the target track through preview and commit, while `TimelineTrackRow` shows the target-row group range cue, fades the source-row ghost, and keeps blocked cues for invalid targets.

The previous M6.5 selected group-move range cue slice makes multi-selected clip drags read as one magnetic object, not just several individual clip offsets. `TimelineTrackRow.groupMoveRangeCue` computes the new per-track group span from `TimelineClipGroupMovePlan`, and `TimelineGroupMoveRangeCue` draws a purple dashed band with start/end rails, moved clip count, time delta, displacement count, and snap state. Timeline clip accessibility values now also report group landing positions and displacement destinations during the drag.

The previous M6.5 focus-gated source monitor I/O shortcut slice lets the editor press `I` / `O` to mark the current source monitor playback time without stealing timeline or text-field input. `StudioWorkspaceView` now keeps a separate `sourceMonitorFocused` state, enters it when a source monitor asset is selected or the Viewer is clicked, clears it when `timelineFocused` returns, and hosts hidden `SourceMonitorShortcutButtons` that call `markSourceMonitorInAtPlaybackTime` / `markSourceMonitorOutAtPlaybackTime` only while source monitor focus and source media are active. Command Palette subtitles now tell users that Source Monitor focus enables `I` / `O`.

The previous M6.5 source monitor current-time mark slice lets the editor play or seek a source-monitored asset and mark the visible source time as IN or OUT. `MediaVideoPlayer` now reports AVPlayer seek/current time through `ViewerSurface` and `ViewerPanel`; `StudioViewModel.updateSourceMonitorPlaybackTime` stores it only while source monitor mode is active; and `markSourceMonitorInAtPlaybackTime` / `markSourceMonitorOutAtPlaybackTime` reuse `TimelineSourceRangeMarkPlan` so marks clamp to the selected candidate range and cannot invert. `MediaPanel.SourceMarkedRangeControls` now shows the current source time and visible `I` / `O` buttons, and Command Palette exposes the same actions without adding schema/compiler contracts.

The previous M6.5 cross-track lift delete slice makes `Delete` / Command Palette `選択項目を削除` useful for selections that span multiple tracks without pretending a safe magnetic cross-track ripple contract exists. `TimelineLiftDeletePlan` creates deterministic `remove_segment` operations only, `TimelineDocument.applyingLiftDelete` removes selected clips and transition references without moving followers, and `StudioFeedbackSession.queueLiftDelete` clears stale pending edits before queueing the same remove operations. `StudioViewModel.deleteTimelineSelection` still prefers same-track ripple delete when safe, but falls back to lift delete for cross-track or otherwise non-ripple clip selections. Command Palette copy now explains same-track ripple versus cross-track gap-preserving delete, and search includes lift/cross-track terms.

The previous M6.5 selected-range loop slice expands the `R` review loop from a single selected clip into the current timeline selection. `TimelinePlaybackLoop.range(covering:)` builds one review range across multiple selected clips, and `TimelinePlaybackLoop.transitionReviewRange(timeline:transition:)` builds a short context range around a selected visible transition. `StudioViewModel.canSetLoopPlaybackRangeToSelection` and `setLoopPlaybackRangeToSelectedClip` now prioritize selected transitions, otherwise use all selected clips. Command Palette, Viewer help, and the macOS Transport menu now say `選択範囲`, and command search includes transition/multi-selection terms.

The previous M6.5 same-track multi-selection ripple-delete slice extends Delete/ripple delete beyond a single clip without taking on unsafe multi-track sync behavior. `TimelineRippleDeleteGroupPlan` accepts multiple selected clips only when they live on the same track, emits timeline-ordered `remove_segment` operations plus cumulative downstream `move_segment` operations, and rejects cross-track selections. `TimelineDocument.applyingRippleDelete(group)` updates the in-memory Timeline immediately, while `StudioFeedbackSession.queueRippleDelete(group)` removes stale pending edits and transition references for deleted clips before queueing the new patch operations. `StudioViewModel.rippleDeleteSelectedTimelineClip` now uses that group plan for single and same-track multi-selection deletes, leaving multi-track ripple delete explicitly unsupported for now.

The previous M6.5 Delete-key selected item slice makes the timeline behave more like a normal NLE: timeline-focus `Delete` and Command Palette `選択項目を削除` remove the selected timeline item. A selected transition uses the existing remove-to-cut path. A single selected clip uses `TimelineRippleDeletePlan`, immediately removes the clip from the in-memory Timeline, moves downstream same-track clips forward through the same move operations that will be saved, seeks Viewer/playhead to the deleted clip's former position, and keeps the unsaved Studio patch inspectable. That slice intentionally avoided multi-track delete until the later gap-preserving lift-delete path could be added.

The previous M6.5 timeline selection shortcut slice makes selection state easier to enter and exit without mouse-only cleanup. Timeline-focus `Command-A` and Command Palette `タイムラインを全選択` now select every timeline clip, seek the playhead to the first selected clip, and clear source-monitor/transition/drag preview state. Timeline-focus `Esc` and Command Palette `タイムライン選択を解除` now clear clip selection, selected transitions, Multi-select, Blade, and transient preview state so the editor can quickly return to ordinary timeline editing.

The previous M6.5 timeline marquee range-selection slice makes Multi-select mode less click-heavy: dragging an empty timeline lane while Multi-select is on now draws `Timeline.SelectionMarquee` and selects every clip on that track whose range intersects the drag span. Normal empty-lane drag still scrubs the playhead when Multi-select is off. This is selection-only state, so it does not create patch operations or schema churn.

The previous M6.5 snapping toggle slice adds a pro-editing control for magnetic behavior: toolbar `吸着`, timeline-focus `N`, and Command Palette all toggle timeline snapping while leaving it on by default. When snapping is off, clip body drag, edge drag trim, ruler scrub, empty-lane scrub, overview-strip scrub, and source candidate card drops pass a zero snap threshold into the existing planners/resolvers, so cursor position wins and snap badges/rails do not appear. When snapping is back on, the existing edit-point/playhead/marker/timeline-start snap behavior returns without changing any patch/schema contract.

The previous M6.5 Blade tool slice adds FCPX-like click-to-split direct manipulation: toolbar Blade, timeline-focus `B`, and Command Palette all toggle Blade mode; while enabled, timeline clips show an orange scissor cue and a click overlay that maps the clicked x-position to a safe split frame. `StudioViewModel.bladeSplitTimelineClip` reuses `TimelineSplitPlan`, queues the existing `split_segment` operation, updates the in-memory Timeline/Viewer immediately, moves the playhead to the split frame, selects the generated right clip, and highlights both sides. Existing split-at-playhead now uses the same helper, so toolbar split no longer waits for save/update before the visible timeline changes.

The previous M6.5 clip-edge drag extension slice changes selected-clip edge drag from one-way trimming into bidirectional resize: dragging the start or end edge inward shortens the clip, while dragging outward extends into available same-track gap when the source handle exists. `TimelineDragTrimPlan` now carries signed duration deltas, extension metadata, adjacent-gap constraints, and optional asset-duration checks. `StudioViewModel.dragTrimTimelineClip` passes asset duration into the planner, commits the same `trim_segment` plus duration-aware `move_segment` path, and updates Timeline/Viewer immediately. `TimelineTrimPreviewBadge` now distinguishes `+Nf` extension from `-Nf` trim, and the edge handles say they can be dragged both ways.

The previous M6.5 selected-clip nudge slice adds `StudioViewModel.nudgeSelectedTimelineClipEarlier` / `nudgeSelectedTimelineClipLater` and matching can-action properties. `TimelineEditToolbar` now exposes `位置←` / `位置→`, and focus-gated timeline shortcuts add `[` / `]`, so selected clips can be moved by 0.5 seconds without a body drag. The action uses the existing `TimelineClipMovePlan` / `TimelineClipGroupMovePlan` and `move_segment` immediate timeline path, including overlap lane lift and multi-selection movement, then returns the Viewer from source monitor to Program preview.

The previous M6.5 source-bin search/sort slice adds `ProjectMediaSourceBinSort`, `ProjectMediaPreviewStatus.matchesSourceBinQuery`, and `ProjectMediaPreviewSummary.items(matching:query:sort:)`. `StudioViewModel` now keeps transient `mediaSourceBinQuery` and persistent `mediaSourceBinSort`, while `MediaPanel` adds a search field, clear button, sort picker, and filtered result count so source assets can be found by filename/asset/status/path and ordered by source order, name, status, or kind before entering source monitor preview.

The previous M6.5 source-bin slice adds `ProjectMediaSourceBinFilter`, filter/count helpers on `ProjectMediaPreviewSummary`, and persistent `StudioViewModel.mediaSourceBinFilter`. `MediaPanel` now shows a segmented all/ready/video/audio/needs-action filter and thumbnail source-bin rows backed by the existing evidence-first `ProjectThumbnailCache`; source-ready rows can be clicked or buttoned into source monitor preview, selected rows are visibly highlighted, and missing/proxy-needed rows stay visible for action-oriented filtering.

The previous M6.5 source edit commit-feedback slice adds `changedClipIDs` to `TimelineSourceInsertPlan`, `TimelineSourceOverwritePlan`, and `TimelineSourceReplacePlan`, then calls `StudioViewModel.showChangedClipHighlight` after source insert-at-playhead, source candidate drag/drop, source overwrite, and selected-clip source replace commits. Source overwrite also now creates its insert clip directly on the chosen compatible target track instead of resolving it through source insert lane-lift, so overwrite remains a replacement operation while ordinary source drops keep the FCPX/CapCut-style occupied-lane lift behavior.

The previous M6.5 existing-transition drag relocate slice adds `TimelineTransitionRelocatePlan`, `StudioDragPayload.transition`, `TimelineTransitionMoveHandle`, and transition-payload handling in `TimelineTransitionDropDelegate`. An applied transition now has a central move handle; hover over another eligible edit point shows move/magnet cues and Viewer transition preview through `StudioViewModel.previewTimelineTransitionMove`, and drop commits immediately through `moveTimelineTransition` by setting the target transition and cutting the source edit point with existing `set_transition` operations.

The previous M6.5 source candidate magnetic drop snap slice extends `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` with `TimelineSourceInsertSnap`, `proposedTimelineInFrame`, and snap-aware source drop planning. Candidate-card drops now snap to nearby same-track edit points, playhead, markers, or the timeline start before overlap lane-lift is resolved, and `TimelineSourceCandidateDropCue/Ghost` show SNAP plus a source-drop snap rail before mouse-up. `TimelineSourceCandidateDropDelegate` passes the same 12px-equivalent snap threshold to preview and commit, so `dropSourceMonitorCandidateOnTimeline` writes the snapped `insert_segment.new_timeline_in_frame`.

The previous M6.5 source candidate overlap lane-lift slice extends `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` with a source-insert `LaneLift`. When a source candidate is dropped onto an occupied compatible lane, the planner first looks for an open same-kind lane at or above the requested lane, otherwise creates the next V/A/O/C lane. `StudioViewModel.previewSourceMonitorCandidateDropOnTimeline` now uses that same planner for drag-time preview, and `TimelinePanel` shares source-drop preview state across rows so the ghost can appear on the actual lifted target row. Drop commit still uses the existing `dropSourceMonitorCandidateOnTimeline` / `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` / `insert_segment target_track_id` path.

The previous M6.5 source candidate drag-time ghost slice adds `StudioViewModel.previewSourceMonitorCandidateDropOnTimeline` and feeds it through `TimelinePanel` into `TimelineSourceCandidateDropDelegate`. While a source candidate card is dragged over a timeline row, `TimelineSourceCandidateDropGhost` shows the marked-range duration as a ghost clip, `TimelineSourceCandidateDropCue` shows the target timecode, and incompatible lanes turn red before mouse-up. Drop commit still uses the existing `dropSourceMonitorCandidateOnTimeline` / `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` / `insert_segment` path.

The previous M6.5 source overwrite pre-click preview slice exposes `StudioViewModel.sourceMonitorOverwritePreview` from the same `TimelineSourceOverwritePlan` used by the real edit. `TimelinePanel` passes it to each row; `TimelineSourceOverwritePreviewBand` draws the target range, IN/OUT boundaries, and operation summary on the target track; `TimelineSourceOverwriteImpactBadge` marks delete/trim/split clips before the editor presses overwrite.

The previous M6.5 source monitor middle-split overwrite slice extends `MediaPanel.OverwriteSourceAtPlayheadButton`: when the marked source range cuts through the middle of one existing target-track clip, `TimelineSourceOverwritePlan` queues `insert_segment` first, then `split_segment` at the overwrite out frame, then `trim_segment`/duration-aware `move_segment` to keep the original clip as the left remainder. `TimelineDocument.splittingClip` and `TimelineClip.splitting` make the Studio preview match compiler-generated IDs, and `runtime/compiler/patch.ts` now remaps outgoing transitions from the split clip to the generated right remainder.

The previous M6.5 source monitor marked overwrite slice adds `MediaPanel.OverwriteSourceAtPlayheadButton`: the current source candidate marked range can be inserted at the playhead while existing target-track clips are trimmed at overlap edges or removed when fully covered. `TimelineSourceOverwritePlan` keeps this inside existing patch contracts by queueing `insert_segment` first, then `trim_segment`/duration-aware `move_segment` for edge overlaps and `remove_segment` for covered clips. The Studio timeline updates immediately, and `runtime/compiler/patch.ts` removes transition metadata that referenced a removed clip so saved `timeline.json` matches the in-memory overwrite preview.

The previous M6.5 source monitor marked replace slice lets `MediaPanel.ReplaceSelectedClipWithSourceButton` use the currently marked source range instead of the whole candidate. `TimelineSourceReplacePlan` accepts `TimelineSourceRangeOverride`, emits `replace_segment new_src_in_us/new_src_out_us` when the mark differs from the full candidate, updates Studio preview immediately while preserving the target clip's timeline position/duration, and `runtime/compiler/patch.ts` persists the same range into saved `timeline.json`.

The previous M6.5 source monitor draggable range handle slice adds `MediaPanel.SourceMarkedRangeScrubber`: the source candidate card now shows the full candidate range, the marked range, and draggable IN/OUT handles. `TimelineSourceRangeMarkPlan` maps drag fractions to source microseconds while clamping to candidate bounds and preventing IN/OUT inversion, and insert/drop/replace use the marked source range contracts.

The previous M6.5 source monitor marked insert range slice lets `MediaPanel.SourceCandidateCard` show a source candidate's full range and a marked range, then nudge IN/OUT by 0.5 seconds before inserting or dragging to the timeline. `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` accepts `TimelineSourceRangeOverride`, emits `insert_segment new_src_in_us/new_src_out_us` when the mark differs from the full candidate range, and Studio immediate preview stays aligned with compiler persistence.

The previous M6.5 source candidate drag-to-timeline slice lets `MediaPanel.SourceCandidateCard` drag onto compatible timeline lanes: `TimelineSourceCandidateDropDelegate` resolves the drop frame and target track, `TimelineSourceInsertPlan` emits `insert_segment target_track_id` when needed, and Studio immediate preview stays aligned with compiler persistence.

The previous M6.5 audio lane-lift slice extends clip-body overlap drag to audio tracks: `TimelineClipMovePlan` now reuses or creates compatible A lanes for overlapping audio moves, queues the same `move_segment target_track_id` contract used by video lane lift, and keeps Studio immediate preview aligned with compiler persistence.

The previous M6.5 source-monitor replace slice adds `MediaPanel.ReplaceSelectedClipWithSourceButton`: a source-monitored candidate can replace the selected video/audio timeline clip immediately through the existing `replace_segment` contract, and compiler persistence now updates `candidate_ref` to match the chosen candidate.

The previous M6.5 transition removal slice adds `Timeline.EditToolbar.RemoveTransition`: an applied transition can be selected and removed back to `cut`, with the in-memory timeline, Viewer preview state, pending patch operation, and saved compiler behavior all using the same `set_transition` contract.

The previous M6.5 source-monitor slice adds visible insert candidate selection: `MediaPanel.SourceCandidateCard` shows segment/range/role/target/confidence/reason and previous/next buttons, and insert-at-playhead now uses the selected candidate ID instead of a hidden best-candidate choice.

Current macOS Studio implementation read:

- `ContentView.swift` lays out a three-zone workspace: Viewer + Inspector above, Timeline below, FeedbackStatusBar at the bottom, with a command palette, focus-gated hidden timeline shortcuts for transport, edit, zoom, and Blade actions, and Source Monitor focus-gated hidden shortcuts for source IN/OUT marking plus append/insert/overwrite/replace edit actions.
- `ViewerViews.swift` provides playback contract badges, source/timeline preview diagnostics, source monitor titles, AV playback, a lightweight unsaved-transition crossfade overlay, audio monitoring, reverse/forward shuttle controls, selected-clip loop/range button and badge, a playback-rate label, 1-frame step controls, and missing-media actions.
- `TimelineViews.swift` renders tracks, markers, audio cues, waveform overlays, playhead, overview strip with clip distribution/markers/playhead and playhead-locate scroll anchor, direct ruler and empty-track click/drag scrub with timecode badges, magnetic playhead snap indicators for nearby edit points/markers/timeline boundaries, single/multi clip selection, clip hover/selection/drag cues, Snapping toggle state that feeds clip move/trim/source drop/ruler/overview/empty-lane snap thresholds, Blade mode toggle/click overlay/orange scissor cue for direct clip splitting, selected-clip drag trim handles with bidirectional shorten/extend drag-time width/offset preview, magnetic trim boundary/snap indicators, signed trim/extension delta badges, clip-body drag move, clip move landing cues, clip move Viewer-preview callbacks during drag changes, shared source candidate drop ghost/cue/timecode handling across timeline rows with lane-lift target display and source-drop snap rail/badge feedback, source overwrite pre-click preview bands and delete/trim/split impact badges, compact/standard/expanded track density control, vertical target-track ghosts/highlights, blocked vertical target cues, transition preset chips, edit-point drop targets with preset hover preview, idle transition landing guides, transition drop candidate cues, transition drop magnet cues, existing-transition duration grips, transition duration delta badges, applied-transition central move handles, transition move candidate/magnet cues, selected-transition toolbar summaries and remove-to-cut action, context-menu actions, the visible `TimelineViewportControls` for zoom/fit/reset/density state, and the visible `TimelineEditToolbar` for approve/reject, bulk approve/reject, ripple delete, selected-clip position nudge, Snapping, Blade mode, fixed trim, playhead trim, split at playhead, roll trim, slip trim, extend trim, swap, search, apply, and undo.
- `AgentInspectorViews.swift`, `ClipInspectorViews.swift`, `MediaPanelViews.swift`, `QADashboardViews.swift`, `CandidateSwapView.swift`, and `FootageSearchView.swift` cover AI jobs, clip notes, evidence, media readiness, source monitor entrypoints, compact source-bin filtering/search/sort/grouping, source-monitored asset insert-at-playhead, source monitor marked range controls, draggable source range scrubber, source monitor selected-clip replace, source monitor marked overwrite, draggable source insert candidate cards, QA reports, candidate alternatives, and multimodal search.
- `StudioViewModel.swift` now adds selected-clip trim commands that queue existing `trim_segment` patch operations, selected-clip nudge commands that queue existing `move_segment` operations and immediately update Timeline/Viewer, persistent `isTimelineSnappingEnabled` state plus `toggleTimelineSnapping`, drag trim that can shorten or extend through `trim_segment` plus duration-aware `move_segment`, Blade mode state and click-to-split behavior that queues existing `split_segment` operations while immediately selecting/highlighting the generated right clip, playhead trim that queues the same patch family, target-track-aware clip-body magnetic drag move that queues `move_segment`, clip move drag-time Viewer preview that feeds a transient moved timeline/source time/timecode before commit, transition drop that queues `set_transition`, selected-transition removal that queues `set_transition` with `transition_type: cut`, applied-transition relocation that previews and commits through `TimelineTransitionRelocatePlan` with target `set_transition` plus source `cut`, transient transition duration and drop-hover previews that update Viewer before the commit, source monitor insert at playhead that queues `insert_segment` for the visible candidate and marked source range, source monitor append to timeline end that queues the same `insert_segment` path at `timeline.totalFrames`, source candidate drag-to-timeline that queues `insert_segment` with the snapped drop frame, compatible target track, marked source range, and overlap lane-lift target when needed, `previewSourceMonitorCandidateDropOnTimeline` for source candidate drag-time ghost/blocked-lane/lane-lift/snap feedback, source monitor selected-clip replace that queues `replace_segment` for the visible candidate and marked source range, source monitor overwrite at playhead that queues `insert_segment` plus edge trim/move/remove operations and middle-spanning split/trim/move operations for the visible candidate and marked source range, `sourceMonitorOverwritePreview` for the pre-click target range/impact overlay, post-commit changed-clip highlights for source insert/drop/replace/overwrite/append, source monitor edit action command/shortcut routing that reuses the existing append/insert/overwrite/replace immediate paths, same-track and aligned cross-track ripple delete that queues `remove_segment` plus downstream `move_segment`, split at playhead that queues `split_segment`, roll trim that queues four gapless trim/move operations across adjacent clips, slip trim that queues one source-range-only `trim_segment`, extend trim that queues one `trim_segment` plus one duration-aware `move_segment`, multi-select state for bulk approve/reject, source monitor state for Media-panel preview, persistent `mediaSourceBinFilter`, `mediaSourceBinSort`, `mediaSourceBinQuery`, `mediaSourceBinGroupMode`, `sourceMonitorAssetID`, and `sourceMonitorCandidateID` state for source-bin filtering/search/sort/grouping and source monitor context restoration, visible insert candidate selection, source monitor IN/OUT mark state, draggable source mark updates via `dragSourceMonitorMark`, and insert, 1-frame step backward/forward transport behavior, reverse/forward shuttle playback state, `playbackLoopRange` / `isLoopPlaybackEnabled` state for selected-clip loop playback, `timelinePixelsPerFrame` / `isTimelineFitToWindowEnabled` state for timeline zoom/fit controls, and `timelineTrackDensity` state for vertical timeline density. Selected-clip nudge, clip-body drag, drag trim, Blade split, selected-transition removal, applied-transition relocation, ripple/lift delete, source monitor insert, source monitor append, source candidate timeline drop, source monitor replace, and source monitor overwrite update the in-memory timeline immediately while preserving patch serialization for save/update. `TimelinePlaybackTransport.swift` owns the direction/speed cycle helper and loop range boundary helper. The visible playback timing/sync diff around `playbackAnchorFrame`, `playbackAnchorDate`, and `timelinePreviewSyncClipID` predates the M6 toolbar work; preserve it unless explicitly asked otherwise.
- `MediaPanelViews.swift` now includes a compact source-bin filter menu, selected filter summary, used/unused filters, usage grouping, and `MediaPanel.SourceBinUsedBadge.*`, so the material browser can distinguish assets already present in the current in-memory timeline from unused source material without crowding the panel header, while keeping source monitor, quick insert, and row drag/drop paths unchanged.
- `StudioViewModel.mediaSourceBinUsedAssetIDs` derives source-bin used state from the current in-memory timeline, so unsaved insert/delete/move-style timeline edits can update the source shelf without adding timeline, review patch, or compiler schema state.
- `ProjectMediaResolver.swift` now supports `ProjectMediaSourceBinGroupMode.usage`, grouping the already-filtered source shelf by unused versus used timeline asset IDs with unused media first.
- `StudioFeedbackSession.swift` now has direct helpers for approve/reject, bulk approve/reject, pending trim lookup, and pending split state, so toolbar, context menu, and bottom status state can stay consistent.
- `TimelineRippleDeletePlan.swift` plans same-track ripple delete and same-range cross-track ripple delete as existing patch operations, while rejecting misaligned cross-track ranges so the UI can fall back to lift delete. `TimelineRippleDeletePlanTests` covers remove/move generation, aligned V/A shared-range ripple, misaligned cross-track rejection, and session conflict cleanup.
- `TimelinePlayheadTrimPlan.swift` plans playhead-aware start/end trims as existing patch operations. `TimelinePlayheadTrimPlanTests` covers source/timeline math and boundary rejection.
- `TimelineSplitPlan.swift` plans split-at-playhead and Blade click splits as `split_segment`. `nextClipID(in:)` mirrors compiler-generated `CLP_####` IDs across video/audio clips so Studio preview and saved timeline stay aligned. `TimelineSplitPlanTests` covers source/timeline math, boundary rejection, missing source range rejection, generated ID scope, and immediate preview splitting.
- `TimelineDragTrimPlan.swift` plans selected-clip edge drag resize by resolving the proposed boundary against same-track adjacent bounds, source-handle limits, optional asset duration, and magnetic snap to the playhead, markers, same-track edit points, or timeline start. It supports inward trims and outward extensions from the same drag gesture and exposes signed duration deltas for preview badges. `TimelineDragTrimPlanTests` covers start/end trim math, immediate timeline application, snap, boundary rejection, extension into gaps, overlap rejection, and missing source-handle rejection.
- `TimelineClipMovePlan.swift` plans clip-body drag move with magnetic snap to edit points, playhead, markers, and timeline start. If the target overlaps same-track video/audio/overlay clips, lane lift can reuse or create compatible tracks so the dragged clip can stay where dropped without pushing downstream clips. `preferredTargetTrackID` lets vertical body drags move a clip to an explicit compatible track; if that target is occupied, the plan now lifts the single clip to an open/new same-kind lane instead of blocking, while still rejecting incompatible track kinds. `TimelineClipMovePlanTests` covers edit-point, playhead, marker snap, overlap displacement, video/audio lane lift, explicit target-track move, explicit occupied target lift to open/new lanes, and incompatible explicit target rejection.
- `TimelineTransitionDropPlan.swift` plans video edit-point transition drop as `set_transition`, clamping transition duration to available clip handles and rejecting gapped/audio targets. It also has `TimelineTransitionRelocatePlan` for moving a visible applied transition to another video/overlay edit point, clamping to target handles, applying target `set_transition`, and removing the source with `cut`. `TimelineTransitionDropPlanTests` covers adjacent edit point, short-clip clamp, relocation, duration clamp, same-target rejection, and gapped/audio rejection cases.
- `TimelineSourceInsertPlan.swift` plans source monitor insert-at-playhead and drag-to-timeline from `CandidateBrowserDataSource`: it exposes stable same-asset insert candidates, can use an explicit candidate ID from the source monitor UI, accepts an optional preferred compatible target track for timeline drops and an optional source range override for marked inserts, otherwise prefers unused candidates for the source asset, normalizes unsupported roles to `support`, routes roles to V1/V2/A1/A2, exposes the inserted clip as `changedClipIDs` for immediate post-commit UI feedback, snaps source drops to nearby edit points, playhead, markers, or timeline start, lifts occupied compatible source drops to an open same-kind lane at/above the requested lane or creates the next lane after snap, creates a preview clip in the immediate timeline, and returns the matching `insert_segment` operation. `TimelineSourceRangeMarkPlan` maps source range handle drag positions to source microseconds. `TimelineSourceInsertPlanTests` covers unused-candidate preference, explicit candidate ID selection, unsupported role sanitization, preferred V/A target tracks, incompatible target rejection, occupied source-drop lane lift, new lane creation, source-drop edit-point/playhead/marker snap, snap-before-lane-lift resolution, upward stacking instead of dropping to V1/A1, marked source-range insertion, candidate range bounds rejection, draggable range clamp/inversion prevention, changed clip IDs, and dialogue-to-A1 routing.
- `TimelineSourceReplacePlan.swift` plans source monitor selected-clip replace from the same visible candidate selection, rejects video/audio kind mismatches, accepts an optional marked source range override, updates the target clip in-memory while preserving timeline position/duration and existing edit-point transitions, exposes the replaced target as `changedClipIDs` for immediate UI feedback, and returns the matching `replace_segment` operation with `with_candidate_ref` plus `new_src_in_us/new_src_out_us` when the mark differs from the full candidate.
- `TimelineSourceOverwritePlan.swift` plans source monitor marked overwrite at the playhead from the same visible candidate selection. When a target track is chosen, it creates the inserted clip directly on that compatible target track instead of using source-insert lane-lift, so overwrite remains pinned to the lane being replaced. It emits edge `trim_segment`/`move_segment` operations and covered-clip `remove_segment` operations against the chosen target track, handles middle-spanning overlaps by queueing `split_segment` at the overwrite out frame before trimming the original clip back to the overwrite in frame, and exposes inserted/trimmed/split visible clips as `changedClipIDs` for immediate UI feedback. This preserves left/right remainders without adding a new schema operation.
- `TimelineDocument.swift` now has local UI-state helpers for immediate manual editing: `movingClip`, `trimmingClip`, `splittingClip`, `applyingTimelineMoveOperations`, `applyingTimelineTrimOperations`, `removingClips`, `settingTransition`, `transitionHandles`, and `activeVisualTransitionPreview`. `splittingClip` mirrors compiler split ID generation for Studio preview and keeps outgoing transitions on the generated right remainder. `settingTransition` upserts visible transitions and removes a matching transition when the requested type is `cut`. `StudioViewModel.dragMoveTimelineClip`, `dragTrimTimelineClip`, `applyTransitionPreset`, `adjustTimelineTransitionDuration`, and `removeSelectedTimelineTransition` use these helpers so manual edits show on the timeline immediately while preserving patch serialization for artifact save/update. `ViewerSurface` uses the active transition preview state to overlay the adjacent source during unsaved crossfade review.
- `TimelineRollTrimPlan.swift` plans incoming/outgoing edit-point roll trim for touching same-track neighbors as existing `trim_segment` + `move_segment` operations. `TimelineRollTrimPlanTests` covers incoming-left, outgoing-right, asset-duration bounds, and non-touching neighbor rejection.
- `TimelineSlipTrimPlan.swift` plans selected-clip slip trim as a source-range-only `trim_segment` operation while preserving timeline position and duration. `TimelineSlipTrimPlanTests` covers left/right source shifts, negative source-in rejection, and missing/insufficient asset-duration rejection.
- `TimelineExtendTrimPlan.swift` plans selected-clip start/end extension into available same-track gap as existing `trim_segment` + duration-aware `move_segment` operations. `TimelineExtendTrimPlanTests` covers start/end extension, gap rejection, source-underflow rejection, and missing/insufficient asset-duration rejection.
- `TimelineViewportScale.swift` owns clamped pixels-per-frame, fit-to-window lane width, zoom in/out steps, display labels, track density constants, and overview frame/x/range mapping. `TimelineViewportScaleTests` covers fit width, clamped detail width, percent labels, density height invariants, and overview mapping.
- `ProjectMediaResolver.resolvePreviewStatus` builds a `ProjectMediaReference` from Media-panel preview readiness rows so macOS Studio can show a source-monitor Viewer state without constructing cross-module media references directly.
- `runtime/compiler/patch.ts` and `schemas/review-patch.schema.json` now include `split_segment`, `set_transition`, optional target-track persistence for source inserts, and optional source range overrides for marked source inserts/replaces. The compiler clones the selected clip on the same track for split, preserves metadata/audio policy/candidate refs, splits caption overlays across left/right clips, rejects boundary split frames, remaps outgoing transitions from the split clip to the generated right remainder, upserts adjacent edit-point transitions into `timeline.transitions`, and treats `set_transition` with `transition_type: cut` as removal of the matching existing transition. `replace_segment` now persists the chosen `with_candidate_ref` into `candidate_ref`, honors optional `new_src_in_us/new_src_out_us` within the candidate bounds, and `insert_segment` now creates a missing target track such as V2/A2 or honors a compatible `target_track_id` from source drag-to-timeline while rejecting mismatched target kinds and candidate-outside marked ranges. `remove_segment` also drops transition metadata that referenced the deleted clip, which keeps source overwrite preview and saved compiler results aligned when a covered clip is removed.

## Next

1. Treat scoped M6 as evidence-complete unless a new current-state regression appears. The authoritative audit is `docs/ux/video-os-studio-m6-completion-audit.md`.
2. Continue the next product slice from M6.5 operation feel, M7 AI-assisted edit bench, or M8 media browser/source monitor. Blade click-to-split, timeline snapping toggle, Multi-select marquee range selection, timeline Command-A all selection, Esc selection/tool clear, Delete-key selected item removal, immediate single/same-track multi-clip ripple delete preview, aligned same-range cross-track ripple delete, cross-track lift delete fallback, selected-range loop playback, selected group-move range cue, same-track selected group vertical move with occupied-lane lift, selected-clip edge drag extension, selected-clip nudge, source monitor insert-at-playhead, visible candidate selection, thumbnail source-bin filters/search/sort/grouping/favorites/used-unused/usage grouping, Source Bin hover skim, active named Source Bin collections with picker/create/rename/delete management, persistent source-bin search/source monitor context, source-bin quick drag/cue/quick insert, marked insert/drop range, source monitor current-time IN/OUT marking with focus-gated I/O shortcuts, Source Monitor W/D/R edit action shortcuts, Source Monitor E append shortcut, drag-time source candidate ghosts, source candidate magnetic drop snap, source candidate occupied-lane lift, draggable source range handles, marked selected-clip replace, edge-safe and middle-split marked overwrite with pre-click range/impact preview, selected transition removal, transition preset active drag status, transition preset Japanese labels, transition edit-point click wells, timeline toolbar grouped icon-first density, timeline ruler playhead HUD, timeline overview scrub snap cues, timeline overview viewport window, and applied transition relocation are implemented, while deeper source workflow polish, bulk bin actions, richer thumbnails, and broader storyline-wide cross-track ripple behavior remain larger follow-up work.
3. Keep direct editing representable as `StudioFeedbackSession` / `ReviewPatchDocument` operations first; avoid schema changes unless an edit operation cannot be represented safely. The true split case required an explicit `split_segment` compiler/schema contract because `insert_segment` derives source/track from candidates instead of duplicating the selected timeline clip.
4. Keep the CapCut/FCPX/Premiere benchmark trace in `docs/ux/video-os-studio-user-stories.md` and the canonical UX tracker in sync whenever a pro-editing control changes.
5. Reuse the current retest matrix for any future pro-editing slice: Swift build, Swift tests, `build_and_run --verify`, Computer Use visible-state smoke, and tracker update.
6. Keep AI actions patch-based and inspectable; do not let AI mutate canonical planning artifacts without preview/approval.

12. Trim-to-playhead shortcut feel: select one clip, put the playhead inside it, press `Q` and `W`, run `先頭を再生位置へトリム` / `末尾を再生位置へトリム` from Command Palette, and use the toolbar edge actions. Confirm the start/end trims immediately change clip position/duration, Viewer playback follows the new in-memory range before save/update, pending unsaved state remains, disabled reasons are clear when the playhead is outside the selected clip, and `Q` / `W` only fire with timeline focus rather than while typing in text fields or using the Source Monitor.
13. Toolbar trim suite feel: select clips with enough handles/gaps and use `先頭0.5`, `末尾0.5`, incoming/outgoing roll left/right, `先頭を伸ばす`, `末尾を伸ばす`, `スリップ←`, and `スリップ→`. Confirm each button immediately changes Timeline width/position or Viewer source frame before save/update, the unsaved state remains visible, status copy no longer says the edit only applies later, repeated presses accumulate predictably, and save/update produces the same timeline shape that was visible before saving.
14. Blade mode split feel: turn on Blade from `Timeline.EditToolbar.BladeMode`, press `B` to toggle it, and open it from Command Palette. Confirm multi-select turns off when Blade turns on, clips show the orange scissor cue, clicking near the start/middle/end splits only at safe interior frames, the two resulting clips remain on the timeline immediately, the Viewer/playhead moves to the clicked split frame, the generated right clip is selected while both sides briefly highlight, unsaved save/discard state remains consistent, and turning Blade off returns clip click to selection.
15. Snapping toggle feel: use `Timeline.EditToolbar.Snapping`, `N`, and Command Palette to turn snapping off/on. With snapping on, drag a clip/trim edge/source candidate and scrub ruler/overview/empty lanes near edit points, playhead, markers, and timeline start; confirm snap rails/badges appear and the result lands on the snap target. Turn snapping off and repeat; confirm the same operations follow the cursor/proposed frame without snap rails/badges, then turn it back on and confirm the previous magnetic behavior returns.
16. Timeline marquee selection feel: turn on `Timeline.EditToolbar.MultiSelectMode`, drag an empty V/A track lane across multiple clips, and confirm the translucent `Timeline.SelectionMarquee` appears while dragging, mouse-up selects the intersecting clips, selected highlights, bottom status count, and toolbar count/tracks/range/duration summary match the range, the playhead seeks to the range start, empty-lane drag returns to normal scrub when Multi-select is off, and dragging a clip body still moves the clip instead of starting a marquee.
17. Timeline select-all and clear feel: focus the timeline, press `Command-A`, and confirm all visible clips are highlighted, the bottom status reports the clip count, the toolbar summary shows selected tracks/range/duration without severe truncation, and playback/Viewer seek to the first selected clip. Then select a transition, enable Multi-select, enable Blade, start any transient preview if convenient, press `Esc`, and confirm clip/transition selection, Multi-select, Blade, toolbar summary, and temporary preview state all clear without creating unsaved patch operations. Also run the same two commands from Command Palette and confirm disabled states are understandable when no timeline/selection exists.
18. Delete selected item feel: focus the timeline, select a single clip, press `Delete`, click toolbar `削除`, and run `選択項目を削除` from Command Palette; confirm each path removes the clip immediately, same-track downstream clips move forward, Viewer/playhead seek to the former deleted position, pending unsaved state appears, and save/discard remains consistent. Then select an applied transition and confirm `Delete`, toolbar `削除`, Command Palette `選択項目を削除`, and toolbar `トランジション削除` all return it to cut with Viewer/toolbar state updated.
19. Multi-selection ripple delete feel: turn on Multi-select, use marquee selection on one V/A track to select two or more clips, confirm toolbar `削除` and `リップル削除` are both enabled but visually distinct, confirm `リップル削除` uses the gap-close arrow icon and multi-clip help copy, press `Delete`, toolbar `削除`, and toolbar `リップル削除`, and confirm all selected clips disappear immediately while unselected downstream clips on that same track move forward by the cumulative deleted duration. Then select clips across V/A/O tracks that cover exactly the same continuous time range, confirm toolbar `リップル削除` is enabled, and confirm `Delete`, toolbar `削除`, and toolbar `リップル削除` remove the shared range and move followers on each selected track forward by the same duration. Confirm Viewer/playhead seek to the earliest deleted position, pending unsaved state appears, save/discard remains consistent, same-track gaps not caused by deleted clips are preserved, and the status says the cross-track selection was ripple-deleted. Then select clips across multiple tracks whose ranges do not align and confirm `リップル削除` is disabled or explains same-range-only support, while toolbar `削除` remains available and preserves gaps through lift delete.
20. Selected-range loop feel: select one clip, multiple clips via marquee/Command-A, and an applied transition, then press `R` or run `選択範囲をループ` from Command Palette. Confirm the loop range covers one clip, the full multi-clip selection span, or the transition review range around the edit point; the Viewer seeks into range when needed; the `Transport.LoopRange` badge shows the expected timecode; `Timeline.Ruler.LoopRange` and `Timeline.Overview.LoopRange` bands span the same start/end frames, switch from active green to held secondary when loop is toggled off, disappear when the range is cleared, stay behind playhead/snap/viewport cues, playback wraps at the range edges in forward and reverse, and source monitor mode still blocks timeline loop setup with a clear status.
21. Cross-track lift delete fallback feel: select misaligned clips across V/A/O tracks, press `Delete`, click toolbar `削除`, or run `選択項目を削除` from Command Palette, and confirm selected clips disappear immediately while all unselected clips keep their original timeline positions. Confirm transitions touching deleted clips vanish, Viewer/playhead seek to the earliest deleted clip frame, pending unsaved state appears, save/discard remains consistent, and the status clearly says the delete preserved gaps rather than doing ripple movement. Confirm toolbar `リップル削除` stays disabled or shows same-range-only help in this state, while an aligned same-range cross-track selection uses the magnetic ripple behavior from item 18.
22. Timeline-to-source match-frame feel: select video/audio Timeline clips and run `選択クリップをソース確認` from Command Palette, press timeline-focus `F`, and use `Timeline.ContextMenu.RevealSource.*`. Confirm Viewer switches to Source Monitor, MediaPanel source row becomes selected, the Source Monitor candidate/range matches the Timeline clip's asset/segment/source range when candidate data is available, source time starts near the Timeline clip's current source position, `I` / `O` range edits and `置換` / `上書き` still target the revealed source, and `戻る` returns to Program preview without creating unsaved patch operations. For UX-143 specifically, set a source-bin filter/search that hides the selected Timeline clip's asset, reveal that clip, and confirm the source-bin resets to a visible condition, scrolls the material shelf to `MediaPanel.SourceBinItem.*`, keeps the selected highlight and `確認中` action visible, and still preserves normal search/filter behavior when the user changes conditions manually afterward.
